## Document 022: 2026-11-05_［みずほ経済インサイト］欧州大規模デモが問う財政再建の道_c421a30a

欧州大規模デモが問う財政再建の道 
金融市場が動揺するリスクは当面残存する見通し

○ このところ欧州ではインフレに伴う生活苦や公共サービスの劣化等を背景に各地で大規模抗議活
動が発生している 
○ 大規模デモは緊縮を伴う財政健全化を難しくする要因となる。財政懸念や政情不安は国債利回り
の上昇やユーロ安を招いており、経済への悪影響も懸念される 
○ 来年にかけて、欧州では複数の重要な国政選挙が予定される中、政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金
融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存するだろう

１．欧州で広がる大規模抗議活動 
このところ、欧州では大規模デモが多発している（図表1）。フランスでは、9月以降、教員不足や
学校施設の不備など、教育環境の改善を求める高校生の抗議活動が続いていた。こうした中で、10月
半ばには、物価高への対策や公共サービスの改善を求める市民も加わり、抗議活動は全国規模に拡大
した。 
大規模デモが発生しているのはフランスだけではない。フランスのデモに一部触発される形でベル
図表 1 9 月以降に欧州で発生した主な抗議活動 図表2 EU：生活苦の割合（左）と 
自国が抱える課題（右）

### Page 2

2

ギーやイタリア等でも抗議活動が発生している。また、スペインでは、高齢女性が家賃を支払えずに
強制退去させられたことをきっかけに、住宅不足や住宅価格・家賃高騰等に対する不満が爆発する形
で大規模な抗議活動が発生し、これが遠因となりサンチェス首相は議会の解散に追い込まれた。 
各地で発生する大規模抗議活動の背景には、近年のインフレによる生活苦やこれを受けた政府への
不満がある1。EUにおいて、生活苦を訴える人の割合は低中所得層を中心に2022年以降高まっている
ほか、自国が直面する課題としてインフレや経済状況を挙げる人の割合も高い（図表2）。これらの要
因が、自国政府に対する信頼感の低迷につながっている（図表3）。 
本稿では、生活改善を求めて各国で広がる抗議活動が経済や財政、金融市場に与える影響について
考察する。 
２．大規模抗議活動は財政健全化を阻む要因に 
一連の抗議活動は、2018年にフランスで発生した黄色いベスト運動を想起させる2。当時の参加者は、
自動車に常備が義務付けられている黄色い安全ベストを着用し、燃料税の増税や生活費高騰に抗議し
た。購買力の低下や政府への不満が背景にある点は当時と共通している。異なるのは、政府の追加的
な財政支出の余地が、当時と比べてさらに狭まっていることである。 
欧州債務危機が起きた2010年代前半以降、順調に進捗してきた政府債務の削減は、2020年代に入る
と足踏みしはじめた。パンデミック時の大規模給付やウクライナ戦争後のエネルギー高対策など、危
機対応の財政出動が頻発したためだ。黄色いベスト運動が発生した2018年と比べて政府債務が増加し
たEU加盟国は、27カ国中18カ国にのぼる（図表4）。 
加えて、足元では防衛費増額や社会保障費・利払い負担の拡大など、構造的な財政支出圧力も高ま
っている。各国の次年度予算案を踏まえると、財政赤字は当面、高止まりする公算が大きい（図表5）。
安全保障環境の変化やエネルギー価格の高騰を踏まえ、EUは一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連の追加支
出をEU財政ルールの対象外とする柔軟化措置を設けている。しかし、当該措置を適用しているドイツ
図表3 EU市民の自国政府への信頼度 図表4 EU加盟国の一般政府債務残高の変化 
（2018年～2025年）

### Page 3

3

やベルギーでは、特例対象の追加支出を除いてもなお、財政赤字をGDP比3％に抑制するEUの基準を
超過する見込みである。それでもベルギーでは、連立与党間の予算協議が決裂し、首相が辞意を表明
する事態に陥った。 
フランスを起点に広がった抗議活動は、緊縮を伴う財政健全化の難しさを浮き彫りにしている。2018
年の黄色いベスト運動では、マクロン政権が燃料税増税の撤回や所得支援策の拡充に踏み切ったこと
で、抗議活動が漸く沈静化に向かった。今回は、11月に新たなデモやストライキが計画されるなど、
いまだ抗議活動が収束する見通しは立っていない。各国政府は事態の長期化を回避するため、家計支
援や公共サービス拡充などの対応を迫られる可能性があり、財政拡張圧力が一段と強まることが懸念
される。 
３．政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存 
金融市場は、欧州主要国の財政状況に対する警戒を強めている。 
フランスをはじめとする高債務国では、政治や財政を巡る混乱を嫌気して、10年債利回りが上昇し
ている（図表6）。2010年代の欧州債務危機を受けてECB（欧州中央銀行）は、条件付きで対象国の国
債を無制限に購入可能なOMT（Outright Monetary Transactions）や経済の基礎的条件では正当化されな
い国債市場の混乱が金融政策の波及を阻害する場合に発動可能なTPI （ Transmission Protection 
Instrument）などの市場安定化措置3を整備してきた。当時よりセーフティーネットが整備されている
こともあり、足元の国債利回り上昇が欧州債務危機時のような金融危機につながる可能性は現時点で
は低い。 
とはいえ、金利の上昇は景気下押しや、さらなる財政悪化の要因となる。金利上昇幅が大きいフラ
ンスを例に足元の利回り上昇幅を踏まえて試算すると、設備投資・住宅投資が1年後に3％程度減少す
るとの結果が得られる（図表7）。金利上昇やそれに伴う景気減速は、利払い費の増加や税収の減少を
図表5 主要国の財政収支実績と見通し 図表6 ユーロ圏の高債務国と 
ドイツの10年債利回り

きなかった国。数値は成立前の政府予算案ベース。

### Page 4

4

通じて、財政をさらに悪化させる要因にもなる。 
各国の政情不安や財政への懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営をも左右する可能性がある。足元では、ユ
ーロが主要通貨に対して下落している（図表8）。ユーロ安は、輸入物価の上昇を通じてインフレ率を
押し上げる要因となる。足元のユーロ下落幅を前提に、時系列モデルを用いてユーロ圏のインフレ率
への影響を試算すると、総合インフレ率は0.3％Pt程度、コアインフレ率は0.1％Pt程度押し上げられる
との結果が得られた（図表9）。ユーロ圏では、イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴うエネルギー価格の高騰を背
景に、直近でもインフレ率が加速している。先行きユーロ安圧力が強い状況が続けば、インフレ率に
一段の上昇圧力がかかる可能性がある。しかし、ECBはインフレ圧力が一段と強まっても、状況によ
っては追加利上げに踏み切りにくくなることが想定される。利上げがさらなる長期金利の上昇、国債
市場の不安定化を招くおそれがあるためだ。このように政情不安や財政懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営
を困難にする要因にもなりかねない。 
各国の財政や政治情勢に対する金融市場の懸念は当面残存する可能性が高い。欧州では、今年11月
にスペイン議会選挙、来年にはフランス大統領選挙やイタリア議会選挙など、重要な国政選挙が相次
いで予定されている。大規模デモの発生に加えて選挙も控える中、各国政府は国民に負担を求める緊
縮的な財政政策を例年以上に打ち出しにくくなっている。また、各国の選挙では極右政党やポピュリ
スト政党の勢力拡大が予想され、選挙後の政治はより一層不安定化するリスクがある。このほか、コ
ロナ禍からの経済復興を目的に設立されたEU復興基金の受け取りが終了することも、来年以降の財政
懸念を強める要因になるだろう。同基金の配分が手厚かった南欧や中東欧諸国は、自国の財政負担を
抑えながら公共投資などを通じて経済成長を支えてきたが、来年以降はこうした効果が徐々に剥落す
ることになる。

### Page 5

5

以上のように、2020年代に入ってから、コロナ禍におけるサプライチェーン混乱、ロシアのウクラ
イナ侵攻やイラン情勢の緊迫によるエネルギー高が立て続けに起きてインフレが継続する中、欧州各
国で有権者の生活コスト上昇に対する不満が強まっている。一方、これらの緊急事態に対応するため
財政出動を繰り返してきたことに加え、高齢化に伴う社会保障費の増加、地政学リスクの高まりを背
景とした防衛費増強の必要性、利払い費の増大に直面しており、各国の財政余力は著しく低下してい
る。来年にかけて予定されている主要国の選挙では、極右・ポピュリスト政党の勢力拡大が予想され
ており、政治の混乱に拍車がかかる懸念もある。欧州の政治情勢や財政状況に対する不安が来年にか
けて沈静化するとは考えにくく、国債市場や為替市場が動揺するリスクに引き続き留意する必要があ
ろう。

## Document 041: 20261109_投資週報_期中選舉藍色浪潮，AI基建疑慮升溫

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 11 月 09 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 期中選舉「藍色浪潮」，AI 基建政策風險升溫：11 月 3 日美國期中選舉民主黨奪回參眾兩院，
德州新任州長揚言暫停資料中心建設，市場擔憂電力、用水與環境審查趨嚴，AI 資本支出前景
遭重新評價；加上 Fed 官員釋出續升息訊號，資金撤出高評價半導體與 AI 基建族群，美股科
技股大幅修正。短線政策雜音難消，但 AI 需求本質未變，後續觀察新國會實際立法方向。
◼ 科技財報喜憂參半，台股賣壓沉重、政府出手穩市：蘋果營收優於預期但毛利率受記憶體成本

壓抑，AMD 資料中心營收近倍增、高通財報後走弱。美股賣壓延燒，台股連日重挫、新台幣
貶破 33 元，行政院指示金管會機動因應並研議穩定股市措施，官股行庫與投信持續買超。看
法：短線波動仍大，但基本面與政策支撐有助指數逐步築底。
◼ 美國製造業續擴張，台灣通膨降溫：美國 10 月 ISM 製造業指數升至 55.5，連續第十個月擴
張，惟價格分項仍處高檔；台灣 10 月製造業 PMI 連續 13 個月擴張但速度趨緩，CPI 年增率
降至 2.33%。看法：美國景氣與通膨黏性支撐 Fed 偏鷹立場，台灣央行壓力相對減輕；後續
關注 10 月非農就業、11 月 10 日美國 CPI 及 12 月 FOMC 會議。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線波動較大，中期看好現金流穩健的 AI 受惠企業：科技股中期成長趨勢仍具支撐，
惟短線宜重視個股分化，美國期中選舉帶來的政策不確定性，短期可能使科技股評價承壓，
惟大型雲端業者持續投入 AI 基礎建設，半導體及相關設備需求仍具中期成長動能，科技股整
體基本面仍具支撐，但短期應避免過度集中於高評價、高資本支出及高度依賴外部融資的企

業。優先關注具備技術競爭優勢、穩定營運現金流及較高投資回報能見度的企業。
◼ 中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債仍較具優勢：在美國政策風險升溫及科技股評價調整期
間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源價格的不確定性，現階段不建議延
長存續期間。中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債在兼顧票息收益、利率敏感度與資產配置
穩定性上仍較具優勢。信用債方面，營運現金流充足、槓桿適中及再融資需求可控的發行人
仍較為穩健，對 AI 相關企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合約現金流與債務到期結構。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧；其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行，通膨風險則偏向上行，因此將保留政策彈性，根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線，且創兩年半來最大漲幅，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明，強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，台股創高吸引外資匯入，推升台幣最高來到 31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至，台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

境外人民幣維持偏強格局，主要在 6.7000 上方震盪，臨近周末，人行中間價為 6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來新低，不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至 4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔，市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存，美元指數維持高檔震盪，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險，並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7100

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪走升。週初，因美元指數在 102 附近盤整，帶領 USD/JPY 延
續前一週走勢震盪走升到 158 上方；週間，因缺乏重要經濟數據，市場偏向觀望，
USD/JPY 維持在 158 附近震盪；臨近週末，USD/JPY 曾一度回測 157.50 附近，不
過在美日利差的主導下，USD/JPY 依舊保持偏多的走勢，終場收在 158.31，週線上
漲 84 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、路透短觀調查顯示，受半導
體強勁訂單推動，日本 10 月大型製造業信心升至正 22，創近五年新高；但原料成
本高漲重擊非製造業，信心指數降至正 23。與此同時，此前投下升息反對票的日銀
鴿派委員佐藤綾野立場軟化，表態支持分階段升息，市場對後續正常化預期升溫，
預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。二、日本首相高市早苗表示密切關注匯率與物價
走勢，強調日本已脫離通縮，將完全尊重央行政策獨立性，暗示不反對進一步升息。
面對通膨加劇與日圓走貶壓力，其過往堅持的貨幣寬鬆立場已出現轉向。然而目前
美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震
盪的可能性較高。

國預期指數同步滑落，反映市場對經濟復甦的信心明顯降溫，預計將令歐元持續承
壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並
推升法德公債利差與信用風險，令歐元承壓走弱。相較美國經濟展現韌性且聯準會
維持偏鷹立場，歐洲深陷景氣放緩與財政掣肘，歐洲央行緊縮空間受限。在歐美基
本面與政策前景持續分歧下，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐，後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

年期券自 2008 年以來高點，5 年期指標券 A15108 則未成交，最近成交利率
為 1.82%。

警戒線，且創兩年半來最大漲幅，目前油價仍居高難下，通膨壓力將令央行維
持偏緊的貨幣政策，加上國際債市利率持續走升，台債殖利率恐面臨上行壓
力，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.95%，10 年券區間為 2.0%~2.10%。

## Document 131: 261112_ubs_accton

Source PDF: all report pdf/261112_ubs_accton.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ab 12 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
Accton Technology Corporation
Q4 GM to see strong recovery
Q3 GM  down 2.1pp QoQ on higher component cost
Accton reported Q326 EPS of NT$23.39, below UBS/Consensus estimates on softer
margins. Q326 sales of NT$120.7bn (up 26% QoQ) was already posted, and slightly
below UBSe as its  AI card business (Trainium 3) shifted to consignment model for certain
higher cost components from mid-August. Q326 GM of 17.6% was down 2.1ppts QoQ

due to   higher component cost and ~1.5 months of  consignment, and we believe this
should be temporary as its customer will reimburse them in the subsequent quarters. Q3
OPEX increased by  15% QoQ, primarily due to higher R&D expenses, resulted in OPM of
13.4% (-1.6ppts QoQ). We estimate data center switch accounted for ~37% of Q326
sales (vs 36% in  Q226), while accelerator card was ~50% of total sales (50% in Q226).
Q4  growth on track despite full quarter of consignment

Accton expects Q426F revenue to grow high-single digit % QoQ with potential upside
for low-teens % QoQ growth, subject to component supply (i.e. switch IC) and
customers' year-end inventory adjustment, despite a full quarter of component
consignment (vs 1.5 month in Q3). It sees robust demand from  cloud switch  with the
social media customer revenue contribution exceeded e-commerce customer from
Q326, which is likely to sustain into Q426F. It also noted the social media customer has

secured more switch chipsets from Marvell and Nvidia Spectrum, and continues to
negotiate with Broadcom for better allocation. Accton also sees better unit and content
growth for Trainium 3 as the customer ramps liquid-cooled design, which carry ~20%
higher ASP and better GM due to increased design complexity. We forecast Q426E sales
growth of 9% QoQ, while its GM should recover to 19.9% (+2.3 ppt QoQ) on more
favourable mix and its customer reimbursement on the Q3 prepaid component costs.

Aggressive capacity expansion to support mid-term  growth
Accton announced  US$226 mn (~NT$7.3 bn) new capex on 12 Nov. for capacity
expansion in Taiwan and Vietnam, as well as construction of new fab/cleanroom in
Malaysia and Singapore. It aims to fund the capex through internal cashflow, bank
loans,  and has announced a 10mn shares of rights issuance (~2% dilution) on 22 Oct.
We believe this will drive its overall capacity to grow 40-50% YoY in 2027E, supporting

overall 2027E revenue to grow ~35% YoY, driven by 1) further ramp of Trainium 3 with
higher adoption of liquid cooling, 2) sustained 800G switch demand and migration to
1.6T with new customer addition, 3) increasing contribution from Optical Switches.
Valuation: Reiterate Buy with NT$3,200 PT
We  fine tune our model and raise   2026-28E EPS by 0-2%. We retain  Buy but lower  PT to
NT$3,200 (from   NT$3,688) based on 22x  average 2027-28E PE (from 26x) on overall

market valuation compression. We believe recent concerns over switch IC  shortages and
GM pressure from higher component costs are overdone as we expect Accton's GM to
recover from Q426E. Accton will host its annual investor conference in late November.

Company Description
Founded in 1988, Accton specialises in ODM/OEM solutions for data center switches, AI
accelerator cards, enterprise/ODM switches, carrier access/telecom and others. Accton is one
of the largest vendors of white-box and OCP-compliant switches globally (by sales).

Leveraging deep expertise in high-speed switching, system integration and rapid time-to-
market, it has secured long-term partnerships with major cloud service providers.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Accton using PE methodology.
Key risks include: 1) weaker-than-expected demand for data center switches; 2) slower ramp
of 800G and 1.6T data center switches; 3) market share loss in the AI accelerator card
business; and 4) softer gross margin than expected.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

M
Update
Realtek Semiconductor  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
Realtek remains relatively resilient despite sluggish PC demand, supported by its
diversified product portfolio and platform agnostic strategy. Near-term customer
inventory adjustments appear to reflect shipment timing rather than underlying
demand weakness. Meanwhile, elevated legacy DRAM prices continue to pressure
device BOM costs, potentially delaying broader adoption of edge Al and other

consumer applications
Looking ahead, edge server and edge Al represent structural growth opportunities,
supported by ongoing development in high-speed SerDes and integrated Al
solutions. Realtek also sees opportunities to strengthen its position in higher-end TV
SoCs amid competition. A normalization in memory prices could provide an
additional catalyst for downstream demand recovery.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 227: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명SK하이닉스(000660) HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명SK하이닉스(000660) HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명 - 기업.pdf

Date: 2026-11-04

### Page 1

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
90 
SK하이닉스  (000660) 
2026.11.04

HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명 
[반도체/전기전자] 고의영 
2122-9179 ey.ko@imfnsec.com

업황 우려에 고유 할인까지 겹친 과도한 하락 
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 230만원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. ‘27년 BPS에 목표 
P/B 2.3배를 적용했다[표1]. 이는 상장 이후 평균 P/B의 +2표준편차에 해당한다. 
‘26~’28년 ROE는 각각 102%, 66%, 47%로, ‘26년 정점 이후에도 과거 업사이클 
고점보다 높은 수준이 유지될 전망이다.  
한편, 이익 지속성에 대한 믿음이 공고해져 시장의 시야가 ‘28년으로 향할 경우 
‘28년 BPS 기준 목표주가는 360만원까지 열릴 수 있다[표2]. 
동사 주가는 ‘26년 6월 고점 대비 10월 저점 기준 57% 하락해 과거 다운사이클 
평균 하락폭인 -51%를 넘어섰고, 과거 8~13개월에 걸쳐 진행됐던 조정이 
이번에는 4개월 만에 나타났다. 시장이 ‘28년 이익 급감을 상정하고 있는 것이다. 
그러나 당사는 아래와 같은 투자포인트와 향후 주가 흐름을 제시한다.

‘28년 HBM 가치, 시가총액의 1.3배 
HBM 비중이 높아질수록 동사의 이익 변동성은 낮아질 것이다. 특히 cHBM으로 
갈수록 고객별 수주생산 제품으로 현물시장과 유통 재고가 없고, 생산에 앞서 
물량과 가격이 결정된다. 동사는 이 전환이 업계에서 가장 선두권이다.  
당사는 동사 HBM 매출이 ‘26년 43조원에서 ‘28년 183조원으로 증가할 것으로 
전망한다. 만약, 동사의 ‘26~’28년 HBM 영업이익에 시기별 TSMC P/E(21배, 
15배, 12배)를 적용하면 HBM 가치는 동사 시가총액의 47%, 93%, 135%를 
설명한다[표3]. ‘27년에 이미 HBM 하나만으로 현재 시가총액이 설명된다는 
의미다. 시장이 통상적으로 받아들이는 멀티플 10배를 타겟팅해도 결과는 크게 
다르지 않다.

150만원에 사면 ‘30년 EPS 2배 
동사의 주주환원은 자사주 매입/소각 중심일 것으로 상정한다. 당사는 동사가 현 
정책 기간(‘25~’27년)에 이어 ‘28년 이후에도 FCF의 50%를 환원하고 이 중 
70%를 자사주 매입/소각에 쓸 것으로 가정했다.  
당사 가정대로라면 총주주환원수익률은 현 시가총액 대비 ‘27년 14%, ‘28~’30년 
연 17~24%다[표4]. 
관건은 매입 단가다. 자사주 매입의 주당가치 개선 효과는 P/E가 낮을수록 
커지는데, 현 주가의 ‘27년 P/E는 2.7배다. 현 주가 수준인 150만원에 매년 균등 
매입한다고 ‘30년까지 주식수는 50% 줄어든다.   
그 결과 ‘30년 주당순이익은 매입이 없을 때 대비 2배가 된다[표4]. ‘28년 이후 
이익이 정체되더라도 주당 이익은 계속 늘어나는 구조이며, 이익 지속성에 대한 
의심이 주가를 누를수록 소각 효과는 커진다. 현 주가 수준의 정체가 장기화되기 
어려운 이유다.

‘27년 주가 경로: 회복, 박스권, 재평가 
연말까지는 급락 이후 저점을 높여가는 회복 구간이다. 솔리다임 상장 철회에 이어 3분기 
실적발표에서 ‘26년 주주환원 100조원과 매 분기 자사주 매입 방침이 제시되면서 환원 
의지는 검증되었다. 
‘27년 상반기까지는 ‘28년 불확실성에 대한 우려가 상존할 전망이다. 가격 하락폭은 LTA 
방어력이 확인되기 전까지 블랙박스다. 이 불확실성이 멀티플을 누르는 동안 ‘27년 이익은 
계속 상향되므로 EPS 상승과 멀티플 하락이 상쇄되는 구간이 이어질 전망이다. 이 구간의 
기회비용은 환원으로 보상받는다. 
‘27년 하반기 이후에는 실적 추정의 기준 연도가 ‘28년으로 넘어가고, 신규 팹 물량이 소량 
출하되면서 센티먼트상 우려는 커질 것이다. 그러나 당사는 2H27에 ‘28년 HBM 협의와 
LTA 연장이 지속적으로 확인되면서 이익이 스트레스 테스트 범위 안에서 유지될 것으로 
전망하며[그림2], 이것이 숫자로 확인되는 시점에 순환 할인이 축소되고 재평가가 나타날 
것으로 판단한다.

업사이드 요인 
① AI 투자 지속성 우려로 최근 주가 하락이 가팔랐던 만큼, 이 부분에 대한 우려가 
완화되면 반등의 속도와 폭이 예상보다 빠를 수 있다 
② 향후 주주환원 규모가 예상보다 더 적극적일 경우 추가적인 업사이드가 될 수 있다. 
혹은 주주환원 이후에도 남는 대규모 FCF의 활용 방안도 촉매가 될 수 있다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

## Document 274: AI交易退潮、外资创纪录涌入！印度股市一度收复年内失地，全球资金开始重新押注印度

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI交易退潮、外资创纪录涌入！印度股市一度收复年内失地，全球资金开始重新押注印度.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

随着 AI 交易退潮，印度因 AI 敞⼝较低、以内需驱动⽽成为全球资⾦轮动的受益者，油价回
落也缓解了市场对其经常账⼾⾚字的担忧。 11 ⽉外资净流⼊已超 120 亿美元，有望创单⽉纪
录， MSCI 印度指数上涨逾 11% 。不过，外资全年仍为净卖出，反弹能否持续，最终取决于
企业盈利能否兑现增⻓预期。
摘要：
印度股市正迎来⼀场意外的反转。此前，印度因缺乏 AI 投资敞⼝⽽被全球资⾦冷落，如今却因同
样的原因重新受到⻘睐。随着拥挤的 AI 交易出现松动，全球投资者开始将⽬光转向印度这个以内
需为主要驱动⼒、科技股权重相对较低的新兴市场。
周⼀早盘，MSCI 印度指数⼀度上涨 0.6% ，在本⽉累计上涨 11% 的推动下，短暂收复 2026 年以
来的全部跌幅，随后回吐⽇内涨幅。相⽐之下， MSCI 新兴市场指数 11 ⽉以来已下跌约 10% ，此
前积累的年内涨幅也⾯临被抹去的⻛险。印度股市与新兴市场整体⾛势的分化，凸显出全球资
⾦配置⽅向正在发⽣变化。
资⾦流向也印证了这⼀转变。 11 ⽉以来，外资累计净流⼊印度股市超过 120 亿美元，有望创下单

⽉历史纪录。⾼盛、汇丰、花旗等机构本⽉相继上调印度股市评级。印度股市跻⾝全球本⽉表
现最佳市场之列，卢⽐也成为亚洲表现最好的货币之⼀。
AI 交易退潮，印度低敞⼝反成优势
过去数⽉，印度市场对 AI 投资热潮的参与度较低，⼀度成为其跑输全球股市的重要原因。由于缺
乏⼤型科技股和 AI 产业链企业，印度未能充分分享全球 AI ⾏情带来的上涨红利，资⾦持续流向科
技股权重更⾼的市场。
但随着 AI 交易⽇益拥挤，相关资产遭遇集中抛售，资⾦配置逻辑开始逆转。印度市场较低的 AI 敞
⼝，反⽽使其成为全球投资者分散⻛险、寻找新配置⽅向的选择。
Equinomics Research 创始⼈兼研究主管 G. Chokkalingam 表⽰，印度市场在此前⼀个⽉的悲观
情绪之后，已经具备反弹条件。油价下跌、外资⼤举回流，以及 AI 交易松动后全球基⾦对⼤型
内需市场和多元化投资机会的需求，共同推动了印度股市回暖。
从市场表现来看，这种资⾦轮动已经有所体现。本⽉ MSCI 印度指数上涨逾 11% ，⽽ MSCI 新兴

市场指数同期⼤幅下跌，两者⾛势明显背离。
油价回落叠加盈利改善，基本⾯提供⽀撑
除了全球资⾦轮动，印度市场⾃⾝的基本⾯也在改善。作为原油进⼝⼤国，印度对国际油价波
动较为敏感。近期油价下跌，缓解了市场对印度经常账⼾⾚字扩⼤的担忧，也为卢⽐和股市提
供了⽀撑。
与此同时，企业盈利表现也显⽰出韧性。据 Jefferies Financial Group 数据，MSCI 印度指数成分
股中，约 165 家公司在截⾄明年 3 ⽉的本财年预计实现 16% 的盈利增⻓，⾼于此前两个财年分别
12% 和 10% 的增速。
Emkay Global Financial Services 副研究主管 Avinash Singh 表⽰，企业盈利的稳健兑现正在改
善印度股市的⻛险回报⽐。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

善，也为市场提供了基本⾯⽀撑。
外资加速回流，印度股市反弹能否持续？
资⾦回流与基本⾯改善，正推动国际机构重新看好印度市场。多家机构本⽉相继上调印度股市
评级，美国银⾏则指出，主动型全球基⾦对印度的配置仍接近历史低位，意味着后续外资仍有
进⼀步增配的空间。
不过，短期资⾦流⼊能否转化为持续的市场上涨，仍存在不确定性。尽管 11 ⽉外资⼤举买⼊印
度股票，但从 2026 年全年看，境外投资者仍是净卖出⽅。NSE Nifty 50 指数周⼀下跌近 1% ，年
初⾄今仍下跌超 2% 。
更重要的是，印度股市⽬前受益于 AI 交易退潮、油价回落等外部因素，这些因素能否持续尚不确
定。随着全球资⾦轮动效应逐渐减弱，企业盈利能否继续兑现增⻓预期，将成为决定印度股市
反弹能否延续的关键。

## Document 350: How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres

Source PDF: all report pdf/How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy aﬀordability shaped the midterms. A divided
government will now face pressure to shield consumers from
rising data centre power costs while speeding up new energy
supply. Onshoring and trade protections will continue, despite
higher near-term costs, as energy security remains a priority
Coco Zhang
After the midterm elections, the government will need to minimise data
centre-related costs for consumers, bring new power supply online faster and

protect supply chains. Pictured: Data centre in Ohio
Exit polls from the US midterm elections highlighted a key concern
shaping voters’ decisions: energy aﬀordability. Nearly 90% of
voters said the cost of living was a crucial or major factor in their
vote for Congress, with many pointing to gasoline and utility costs.
Meanwhile, as the AI data centre boom strains power supply and
infrastructure, 56% said the issue was a crucial or major factor in

their vote.
With Democrats now set to control both the House and Senate,
what does this mean for US energy and data centre policy?
Your privacy is important

### Page 2

Newly elected lawmakers will face pressure to address these
concerns, requiring the Democratic Congress and Republican White
House to ﬁnd common ground on shielding consumers from data
centre-related costs and accelerating new power supply, including
through permitting reform. Onshoring and supply chain protections
are also likely to continue. While they can raise near-term costs,
they remain important to US energy security. Developments in all
three areas could have signiﬁcant implications for businesses.

That said, Congress needs to strike a balance when drafting
legislation aimed at achieving these goals. More aggressive
measures could face a veto from President Trump.
Strengthened data centre regulation ahead,
but the devil is in the detail
The data centre industry is facing heightened local opposition.
Recent polls suggest that around one in seven Americans is
concerned about or opposes data centre development in their
neighbourhoods.
In response to this issue, there is likely to be tighter data centre

regulation. State-level policy will be more important to watch, as
action is politically and practically easier at this level. The most
likely approach is targeted regulation, much of which is already
emerging. This includes data centre-related taxes, reduced tax
incentives, environmental standards, and requirements for
developers to bear relevant cost increases. Outright moratoriums
remain unlikely to become mainstream. Where they are introduced,

they are more likely to be temporary, giving states time to develop
a data centre policy framework.
At the federal level, the key unanswered question is how much
authority should remain with the states and whether federal
legislation should mandate state regulations or simply provide
guidance. Republicans generally favour letting states lead, while
Democrats tend to support a stronger federal role.
We think sweeping data centre legislation is unlikely because of

President Trump’s veto power. Federal action is more likely to focus

### Page 3

on aﬀordability by protecting electricity customers from higher
costs and formalising data centres' responsibility to help fund grid
and energy infrastructure upgrades. Such measures could be added
to broader legislation, such as must-pass spending bills needed to
avoid a government shutdown, or a bipartisan package like
permitting reform.
More broadly on AI, we expect the Democratic Party to increase
scrutiny of technology companies, as discussed here, while utilising

the next two years to prepare an AI governance framework for 2028
and beyond.
However, tighter AI and data centre regulation is unlikely to
fundamentally alter the industry’s growth trajectory. AI’s
importance to US economic growth and competition with China will
remain powerful drivers of expansion.
Business implications
State-level policy would more directly aﬀect project siting,
which is important for data centre companies to watch.
Where state rules are tighter, project development could slow.

However, a more restrictive regulatory environment could
also increase the value of data centres already approved or
under construction, easing some of the industry’s capital
constraints. Higher valuations could also encourage
developers to be more selective and expand at a more
measured pace, ultimately improving the quality of the
project pipeline.
Developers should also treat community engagement as a
strategic investment. Recent surveys show that while only

20-30% of Americans support local data centre construction,
support can rise to 50-70% when there are accompanying
community investments, such as in public infrastructure
improvements or reductions in residents' costs. More
developers are now signing binding community beneﬁt
agreements as part of project development.

Finally, sustainability will become more important for project
development, not only to meet potential environmental

Enabling faster power generation through
permitting reform
Both parties support timely expansion of electricity supply to meet

rising demand from AI and manufacturing reshoring. They also
generally agree that energy abundance can help reduce cost
pressures. While Democrats and Republicans diﬀer on the preferred
energy mix, there is still common ground. Technologies likely to
retain bipartisan support include nuclear, geothermal, carbon
capture, and biofuels.
However, the greatest potential for policy action may lie not in
speciﬁc technologies, but in permitting reform.
Today, many energy projects can spend over ﬁve years navigating

the federal permitting process. Around $240bn to $280bn of
infrastructure investment across the eight sectors below enters the

With both parties recognising the need for reform, the Senate
introduced the Bipartisan American Aﬀordability and Jobs Act
(BAAJA) in September 2026. Key permitting proposals include:

Speeding up approvals for energy projects
Providing greater certainty and consistency across
administrations
Making it easier to permit power transmission lines
Despite debate over its environmental impact, there is broad
agreement that the BAAJA would meaningfully improve the
permitting process. The key question is whether it can become law
under the current Congress. The Senate is expected to vote on 9
November, after which the bill would still need approval from the

House and President Trump. Democrats may want to delay the bill
and seek changes once they regain control of Congress next year,
but doing so could increase the risk of a presidential veto. This
creates an incentive to reach a deal sooner rather than later.

### Page 6

Even if the BAAJA does not pass under the current Congress,
permitting reform is likely to remain a bipartisan theme. The
growing need to expand power supply and infrastructure more
quickly will keep pressure on future Congresses to streamline
permitting.
Business implications
For energy companies – both renewables and fossil fuels –
the BAAJA could signiﬁcantly shorten permitting timelines. If
enacted, it would give project developers greater certainty,

reducing the risk of litigation and permit cancellations. This
could further expand project siting options for developers.
Nevertheless, in the very near term, projects may need to
retain ﬂexibility around their permitting strategy while the
new policy framework is developed and implemented.
Expect more protectionism and reshoring
efforts against China dominance
From clean energy technologies to the critical minerals that
underpin much of the US economy, China controls a large share of

global production capacity. Consequently, reducing reliance on
China will remain a US policy priority. Such eﬀorts would increase
rather than reduce project costs in the near term. But the long-term
beneﬁts of stronger domestic manufacturing and more secure
supply chains could outweigh those costs.
Global supply chain capacity distribution
across various sectors

### Page 7

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF, International Energy Agency
This creates two key risks. First, heavy reliance on China leaves US
industries vulnerable to geopolitical tensions. We have already seen
this with rare earths, where Chinese export restrictions contributed
to disruptions for US auto manufacturers. Second, China’s supply
chain dominance can put US manufacturers at a competitive
disadvantage.
Building more secure energy and natural resource supply chains

has long attracted bipartisan support in the US. The Trump
administration has supported domestic rare earth production
through funding, purchase agreements, and equity investments. In
solar, China-focused trade and tax credit restrictions are also
supporting the expansion of domestic manufacturing.
We expect this focus to continue. The executive branch could
pursue further protective measures and is unlikely to face major
opposition from Congress. Congress could also authorise additional

funding to strengthen rare earth supply chains.
Furthermore, a Democratic-controlled Congress could push to
extend clean manufacturing tax credits as part of broader
negotiations with President Trump. Given the administration’s focus
on countering China and expanding US manufacturing, an
extension could emerge as a compromise in a broader legislative
package under an optimistic scenario. All these eﬀorts would also
extend into future administrations.

Business implications
For US companies:
Clean energy companies and other industries that rely on
rare earths, including semiconductors, AI, defence, and

electronics, should have enough policy certainty to build
long-term supplier relationships outside China. In the near
term, however, diversiﬁcation could raise supply chain costs
as alternative sources may be more expensive. Solar could
face particular cost pressures, as some tariﬀs on supply
chain equipment also apply to non-Chinese suppliers.
For overseas companies:
US eﬀorts to build supply chains outside China are creating
opportunities for manufacturers elsewhere. The US is

supporting rare earth production in Australia and Brazil, while
solar and battery suppliers in countries such as South Korea
and Japan could beneﬁt from stronger demand. This could
also encourage more manufacturing investment in the US.
Meanwhile, European clean energy developers could face
greater competition for non-Chinese supplies. While the EU
has not imposed US-style tariﬀs, it is tightening restrictions
on Chinese equipment through funding, procurement,

auction, and subsidy rules. This could intensify global
competition for non-Chinese suppliers and push costs higher.

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

### Page 7

セン
To
◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

(%)
The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

Figu

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.

## Document 365: LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI  investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

LatAm’s silicon dividend: 
tailwinds from the AI  
investment boom in the U.S.

1 
Authors 
Fancisco Campos 
Chief LatAm Economist

Beatriz Garcia Nunes 
LatAm Economist

October 28, 2026 
Key messages: 
- U.S. investment has broken away from Europe and China since 2023, 
powered by AI-related equipment, software and research spending. 
- The AI CapEx boom demands commodities and merchandise that LatAm 
can supply.  
- Latin America’s export gains are real but concentrated. Copper, computer 
and electronics products provide the clearest links to the U.S. AI buildout. 
- Chile is the region’s most direct copper beneficiary. Mexico captures the 
windfall stemming from increases hardware demand and trade-diversion. In 
these cases, the AI-related tailwinds have undergirded appreciable 
improvements in external accounts. 
- The cases for nickel and zinc illustrate that the dividend is not widespread. 
U.S. imports of the former have mostly returned toward pre-pandemic levels, 
while for the latter bilateral gains partly reflect supplier substitution. 
- While expanding AI use, supply shortages and incentives can extend the 
CapEx boom, its financial viability poses downside risks. 
Another ‘American exceptionalism’ story  
Investment has become one of the sharpest dividing lines among the world’s 
core economic blocs. Since 2024, U.S. gross fixed capital formation has firmed 
up appreciably. Meanwhile, investment in Europe has broadly stalled while in 
China’s it has retreated, the drivers of which are beyond the scope of this report 
(Figure 1).  
The U.S. divergence has largely been an AI story: a burst of spending on 
computing power, digital infrastructure and intellectual property has offset 
weakness in conventional construction and housing. Indeed, total U.S. fixed 
investment is nearly 12% above its 2023 level. Within it, investment in 
information-processing equipment has risen by roughly 57%, software outlays by 
about 35%, and research and development by around 15%. By contrast, non-
residential structures and residential investment sit below their 2023 levels 
(Figure 2).

### Page 2

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: The global investment divergence

Figure 2: AI-related categories lead U.S. investment

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Eurostat, National Bureau of Statistics of 
China, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Latin America’s commodity channel 
AI is not merely a semiconductor story. Chips require specialized minerals; data 
centers require electricity and cooling; electricity requires grids, transformers and 
storage. That chain pulls in copper, aluminum, silver, tin, lithium and selected 
semiconductor inputs, along with natural gas and uranium. Latin America has 
meaningful exposure to several of these inputs, but copper has emerged as the 
commodity with the cleanest and most economically significant regional link. 
Copper is used throughout the AI infrastructure stack, including power cables, 
substations, transformers, server boards, heat sinks and cooling systems. Rising 
U.S. copper demand has lifted imports from Chile and Peru, particularly the 
former (Figure 3). The effect matters beyond merchandise export volumes. 
Favorable terms-of-trade have helped Chile generate a current-account surplus 
for the first time in roughly fifteen years (0.2% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-
quarter rolling basis) and have allowed Peru to log the largest surplus in about 
forty-five years (4.5% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis).

28 October 2026

### Page 3

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Figure 3: Copper epitomized the commodity link

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Not every metal is copper 
The broader mining story demands more caution, though. U.S. nickel imports have 
recovered toward pre-Covid levels, but the upswing has not displayed the same 
persistence as copper. Mexico has captured a disproportionate share of the 
bilateral improvement, yet that gain looks more like normalization than a 
sustained AI-driven break (Figure 4). Zinc tells a similar story. U.S. real imports 
stand shy of their pre-pandemic levels, and stronger purchases from Mexico and 
Peru partly reflect a reallocation away from Brazil rather than a decisive increase 
in U.S. demand (Figure 5).

Figure 4: Nickel: recovery, not a boom

Figure 5: Zinc: a substitution story

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

28 October 2026

### Page 4

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

Mexico’s hardware advantage 
The larger non-commodity prize is in computers and electronics. U.S. imports of 
these products have sky-rocketed, while the supplier mix has shifted away from 
China. Mexico and various Asian economies have gained share on the back of this 
double-whammy impulse (Figure 6). 
This helps explain why Mexico’s non-automotive manufacturing exports, notably 
electronics, have outpaced automotive shipments to the U.S. and pressures from 
higher oil prices. The resulting non-oil trade surplus has offset pressure from the 
oil balance and helped Mexico generate a balanced current account (0% of GDP 
as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis). The external boost is meaningful, 
although it should not necessarily be interpreted as bona fide evidence of 
nearshoring, in our view.

Figure 6: U.S. computer & electronics imports going into overdrive

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research.

How much runway remains? 
Quantifying or mapping out a timeframe for the AI investment outlook is beyond 
the scope of this piece or our expertise. Nevertheless, we’d venture to argue that 
the capex cycle appears more likely to mature gradually than to stop abruptly. 
One reason is that the investment case is shifting from frontier-model training 
toward enterprise inference. Capacity that once served development can 
increasingly run commercial workloads for companies. That creates a path to 
revenue even if artificial general intelligence (AGI) crystallizes later than 
expected. It also spreads demand to a much larger corporate customer base.  
A second reason is scarcity. Graphics processing units (GPUs) remain tight, while 
power availability, grid connections, cooling equipment, construction capacity 
and data-center complexity limit how quickly supply can respond. Bottlenecks 
may therefore extend the cycle. The constraint is especially relevant for copper 
28 October 2026

### Page 5

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

and power infrastructure, where projects have long lead times and permitting 
hurdles. 
A third reason is strategic. AI development resembles a prisoner’s dilemma at 
both the corporate and national levels. Each participant may worry about costs, 
safety and control, yet slowing unilaterally risks surrendering technological, 
commercial or national security advantages. The same tension is visible in calls 
for guardrails alongside continued spending. Competition can therefore sustain 
capex even when short-term returns are uncertain. 
We acknowledge the risks, nonetheless. Higher borrowing costs raise the hurdle 
rate for an infrastructure buildout financed partly through debt. More important, 
revenues must eventually justify the installed capital base. The bearish case is not 
that AI lacks value; it is that infrastructure spending may run ahead of 
monetization, compromising some companies’ repayment capacity. Public 
opposition to the development of AI infrastructure and/or adoption of AI 
technologies could also slow down the technology’s roll-out. 
The regional balance  
Summing up, the AI investment boom in the U.S. is delivering a dividend to parts 
of Latin America, not the region as a whole. Chile and Peru benefit through copper 
prices and export volumes. Mexico benefits through electronics manufacturing 
and the replacement of Chinese supply. For Brazil and Colombia, the AI dividend 
is rather muted. Where materialized, furthermore, the dividend supports trade 
and current accounts more clearly rather than improving the domestic 
investment. 
These dynamics should persist while U.S. AI investment remains supply-
constrained and strategically protected. If widespread adoption advances and 
the strategic race intensifies, the impulse can last. If financing costs rise further 
or monetization disappoints, the cycle may narrow. Either way, copper and 
Mexican electronics remain the region’s most visible gauges of the U.S. AI 
investment appetite.

28 October 2026

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
LatAm’s silicon dividend: tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. – October 28, 2026

ITEM DEFINITIONS AND METHODOLOGY
3© 2026 LSEG.  All marks herein are used under licence. TRPR_82222_875
General:
All aggregates are bottom-up aggregates calculated from analysts' consensus forecasts unless otherwise noted. Bottom-up
aggregates are weighted calculations using the I/B/E/S data from LSEG share class monitored or the official index weight of
the index-provider. Top-down forecasts are compilations of strategists' estimates. I/B/E/S universe calculations are based on

all companies within a given market monitored by LSEG.
Benchmark index calculations, including those for official local market indices, the S&P 500 Index, and the FTSE World Equity
Indices are calculated using official index constituents and weightings. All index composites are representative of current
index constituents. Index constituents and weightings are updated monthly during options expiration (the Thursday before the
third Friday of every month) for every index except the S&P 500, the constituents of which are current as of the date of

calculation.
PEIGBY refers to the Price Earnings Inverse Government Bond Yield ratio, which measures equity valuation with respect to
the inverse-yield on long-term government bonds (now known as the 'Fed' model). All PEIGBY ratios are I/B/E/S universe-
specific except for the US ratio, where it is based on the S&P 500 Index.
Methodology:
For US and selected Canadian aggregates, calendar-year aggregation is performed using the sum of four quarters' estimates

and a quarterly pro-ration is applied to non-December year end companies' quarterly EPS estimates. Outside of the US and
Canada, all company-level data are calendarized prior to aggregation using the Compustat method of calendarization.
Aggregation is performed using the annual consensus EPS estimate and a May roll-back is applied to December year-end
markets. Aggregates for Japan, where most companies report in March, use an August rollback and a November rollback is

used for Australia and New Zealand, where the fiscal year-end for most companies is in June.
All 12-month-forward data for ex-US markets are calculated using a pro-rata weighting to annual EPS forecasts. The
weightings applied are a daily calibration out of 365 days relative to each constituent's individual fiscal year-end and the
current date.
All non-surprise data, including aggregate forecast earnings, forecast growth, and forward-looking valuation calculations are

weighted according to the I/B/E/S share class monitored for I/B/E/S universes or the weightings assigned to each constituent
in a given index. Aggregated earnings calculations are the equivalent of net profit aggregates, as they are derived from the
EPS forecasts aggregated to the net income level by the appropriate share weighting.
Earnings Aggregation:
For 12-month-forward earnings, the 12-month-forward EPS is used in place of the calendarized EPS in the formulas below.

I/B/E/S universe EPS Aggregate = Index EPS Aggregate =
Growth Calculations:
All growth calculations are the weighted year over year growth in earnings for the calendarized fiscal year period. The
formulas below use index shares for index growth calculations.
Calendarized Earnings Growth = 12-month-forward Growth =
Valuation:
All valuation calculations are the weighted-average price-earnings ratio for the fiscal period in question. The formulas below

use index shares for index P/E calculations.
Calendarized P/E Aggregate = 12-month-forward P/E =
Revisions Momentum:
Unless otherwise indicated, analysts' revisions momentum is a simple aggregation of the total number of estimates raised
divided by the total number of estimates lowered. Thus, any revisions ratio less than 1 indicates negative revisions
momentum while a ratio greater than 1 indicates positive revisions momentum, with 1 marking parity.

The Global Aggregates from LSEG product contains annual aggregates for universes included in this publication, including
supplemental aggregate data of over 100 items as well as history dating back to 1985 for North America and to 1987 for other
markets. To learn more about this product, contact your LSEG sales representative.
FTSE and the FTSE World Equity Indices are owned by FTSE International Limited.

## Document 424: US Economics Analyst_ 2027 US Outlook_ The End of the Inflation Shocks

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Economics Analyst_ 2027 US Outlook_ The End of the Inflation Shocks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 20
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The main economic surprise of 2026, the energy shock from the Iran war, has not n
prevented the US economy from growing at a healthy pace but has reversed 
progress on inflation.  The key drivers of growth and inflation in 2026—the war, 
tax cuts, tariffs, and AI price pressures—should all have less impact next year.  
We expect this to leave 2027 GDP growth at an above-consensus 2.4% and the 
labor market stable, while allowing core PCE inflation to fall substantially to 2.4%. 
Growth should follow the same pattern in 2027 as in 2026: solid consumption n
growth and strong business investment growth offset by drags from housing and 
trade.  Consumption should grow a bit over 2%, driven mainly by firmer and more 
evenly balanced real income growth as inflation moderates and the boosts from 
last year’s tax cuts and wealth effects diminish.  AI drives half of our 8% forecast 
for business investment growth but contributes only modestly to measured GDP 
growth because much of the equipment is imported or undercounted in the GDP 
statistics.  Housing investment will likely contract again as poor affordability 
continues to depress demand. 
Near-potential growth should keep the labor market stable in 2027.  This year n
began with fears of a looming AI-driven layoff wave, but the layoff rate instead 
remained historically low, and the labor market settled into a slightly softer 
low-hire, low-fire form of full employment where wage growth has stabilized at a 
subdued pace of about 3.5%.  We expect this equilibrium to last for another year 
before AI displacement begins to have a larger impact in subsequent years. 
While the near-term inflation outlook remains uncertain, we are more confident n
that inflation will fall substantially by late 2027 as the effects of tariffs, the Iran 
war energy shock, and AI price pressures diminish.  We see these three forces as 
the key to the outlook because the economy is not overheated at a 
macroeconomic level or even at an industry level outside of pockets tied to the 
AI boom, and high inflation does not appear to have become entrenched in 
expectations.  As a result, inflation should moderate as long as these forces 
subside and no new shocks emerge. 
The Fed is likely to remain firmly focused on inflation in 2027.  We forecast two n
more 25bp rate hikes in December 2026 and March 2027 to a peak of 
4.25-4.5%, followed by gradual cuts back to 3.25-3.5% starting in November, the 
final meeting of 2027, once inflation has declined meaningfully.  The FOMC will 
also discuss the conclusions of the task forces next year, but we expect any

### Page 2

institutional changes to be limited.  In particular, a large reduction in the size of the
Fed’s balance sheet is unlikely, though a shift in the composition of its assets is
possible.
The most obvious risk to our forecast of sturdy growth and a long-awaited decline in n
inflation is that the inflation shocks of the last two years, which we and most
forecasters did not anticipate, could last longer, or new shocks could arise.  We also
see downside risks to growth from the war, a longer period of higher interest rates,

or earlier and more rapid job displacement from AI.  But our best guess is that there
will be fewer big policy-driven macroeconomic shocks in 2027.

### Page 3

2027 US Outlook: The End of the Inflation Shocks

The main economic surprise of 2026, the energy shock from the Iran war, has not 
prevented the US economy from growing at a healthy pace.  We expect GDP growth of 
2.5% in 2026 Q4/Q4, in line with our forecast from our 2026 Outlook but above both 
consensus expectations at the time and our forecast after the war began.  In contrast, 
the combination of the energy shock and price pressures sparked by the AI investment 
boom are likely to leave core PCE inflation at 3.2% on a Q4/Q4 basis, a disappointment 
to the more optimistic expectation for a large decline that we and consensus held at the 
start of the year.

The key drivers of growth and inflation in 2026—the war, tax cuts, tariffs, and AI price 
pressures—should all have less incremental impact in 2027.  We expect this to leave 
2027 Q4/Q4 GDP growth at 2.4% (above consensus at 2.2%) and the labor market 
stable, while allowing core PCE inflation to fall to 2.4% (in line with consensus).

### Page 4

The Growth Outlook: Solid Consumption, Strong Business Investment, 
and Weak Housing Balance Out to Near-Potential GDP Growth 
Growth should follow the same pattern in 2027 as in 2026: solid consumption growth 
and strong business investment growth offset by drags from housing and trade.  We 
expect this to balance out to overall GDP growth that is a hair above our 2.3% estimate 
of potential growth.  Relative to consensus, our growth forecast is a touch higher on 
consumer spending, much higher on business investment, and notably weaker on 
housing and net exports.

### Page 5

The resilience of the US economy this year in the face of the energy shock has been 
particularly impressive in the case of consumer spending.  Despite a boost to headline 
inflation that has left real wage growth close to zero this year, consumer spending has 
grown at a strong pace, supported by tax refunds, a positive wealth effect from the 
stock market, and a modest decline in the saving rate. 
Our consumer outlook for 2027 is less complicated: we expect 2.2% growth in consumer 
spending driven mainly by stronger real labor income growth as inflation moderates, 
with special factors playing a more limited role.  We do not expect a meaningful change 
in the saving rate, which is likely close to 5% after accounting for revised spending and 
income data that are incorporated with a lag, putting it roughly in line with what one 
would expect at a time when household wealth is high and layoff risk is low. 
Both real income and consumer spending should grow more evenly across the income 
distribution in 2027.  While we noted last year that real income was actually more in line 
with pre-pandemic trends across income quintiles than the K-shaped narrative 
suggested, in 2026 income growth has been weaker for low-income families, due in part 
to fiscal policy changes and the unequal burden of the energy shock.  Income growth 
should even out in 2027, with a boost at the bottom from lower inflation, cost-of-living 
adjustments to government transfer payments, and the likely reversal of recent cuts to 
social spending.

AI will remain the dominant driver of business investment growth in 2027.  We estimate 
that spending on AI-related hardware and intellectual property will exceed $900bn 
annualized by end-2026 (a $550bn increase from 2025) and $1.4tn by end-2027 (a 
further $500bn increase), roughly 4% of GDP. 
The midterm election results should pose only modest risks to AI investment in 2027.  
We expect newly elected Democratic governors in states that account for over a third of 
planned US data center capacity to impose moratoria on new data centers.  But these 
are unlikely to be long-lived and should only affect projects not yet permitted, leaving 
the vast majority of planned 2027 construction unaffected.

### Page 6

The impact of AI on the official economic statistics will be smaller than company 
spending data suggest, for a few reasons.  First, spending will rise less in real than in 
nominal terms because technology goods prices are likely to rise further.  Second, 
semiconductor investment is undercounted in the business investment statistics.  Third, 
most of the technology goods used for AI are imported and are therefore netted out in 
the GDP calculation. 
Despite the undercounting, AI drives half of our 8% forecast for business investment 
growth in 2027.  Its impact on GDP growth is more nuanced: we estimate it will 
contribute 0.2pp to officially measured GDP growth; 0.7pp to true GDP growth, adding 
back undercounted semiconductor investment and exports of chip design services; or 
0.6pp if we also include positive and negative spillovers such as wealth effects and 
limited crowding out of other investment.  One implication of these calculations is that 
growth in the rest of the economy—GDP growth minus the 0.2pp measured impact—is 
not as weak as often claimed.

### Page 7

Housing is likely to remain the weakest part of the economy in 2027 as very poor 
affordability continues to depress demand.  Single-family homebuilding has remained 
close to pre-pandemic levels so far even though mortgage rates are now 3.5pp higher.  
The main reason has been the housing shortage, which has meant that despite worse 
affordability, demand has remained sufficient to absorb the limited supply available 
without a large decline in home prices, which has sustained the incentive to build.  But 
with the housing shortage now less acute and margins for homebuilders normalizing, 
homebuilding is likely to become more sensitive to changes in interest rates.  Higher 
interest rates will also continue to dampen housing turnover by locking in homeowners 
who have low interest rates on their mortgages.

### Page 8

The Labor Market Outlook: A Slightly Softer Form of Full Employment 
While 2026 began with fears of a looming AI-driven layoff wave, jobless claims instead 
fell even further and the layoff rate remained historically low.  Coupled with a slow pace 
of hiring but a stabilization in job openings at a healthy level, this has left the labor 
market in a slightly softer low-hire, low-fire form of full employment.

Our slack tracker—a composite of ten measures of labor market tightness—likewise 
signals that the labor market is a touch softer than the unemployment rate alone 
suggests.  We expect the current equilibrium to persist in 2027, with job growth 
remaining healthy as the economy grows near potential. 
But companies will eventually want to use AI to reduce labor costs more meaningfully, 
and we expect faster displacement of workers from AI-exposed jobs to gradually push 
the unemployment rate higher in subsequent years.  The early evidence on how 
disruptive this will be is mixed.  The labor market already looks tougher for workers 
displaced from AI-exposed jobs, many of whom are moving into roles with similar or 
higher AI exposure that leave them vulnerable.  But young people, who have tended to 
adapt better historically, are already adjusting by shifting to college majors less exposed 
to AI displacement.

### Page 9

The convergence of our wage growth tracker and survey-based wage growth 
expectations suggests that wage growth is stabilizing at about 3.5%.  With productivity 
growth running above 2% this cycle, this implies subdued inflationary pressure from 
labor costs.  We see little reason to expect wage growth to accelerate meaningfully next 
year because the labor market is not unusually tight and workers appear to be aware 
that finding a new job is harder than usual and concerned about displacement risk from 
AI.

### Page 10

Shock, and AI Price Pressures 
The bulk of the upside surprise to inflation in 2026 came from the Iran war energy shock 
and AI price pressures, and the effects of those two forces and tariffs remain central to 
our inflation forecast for 2027.  The economy is not overheated at a macroeconomic 
level, and our bottlenecks tracker suggests it is not overheated at an industry level either 
outside of pockets tied to the AI boom.  Nor does high inflation appear to have become 
entrenched in expectations.  As a result, inflation should moderate as long as the effects 
of tariffs, the war, and AI price pressures diminish and no new shocks emerge.

The impact of earlier tariff rounds on consumer prices appears to be over, and the 
impact on year-over-year inflation should continue to shrink as the base effects from 
last year drop out.  We expect the White House to replace some of the tariffs that were 
ruled unconstitutional by the Supreme Court, but this should have only a modest impact 
because the tariff increase will likely be small and our analysis indicates that removing 
tariffs had a minimal effect on consumer prices, so restoring them should too.

### Page 11

The outlook for the Iran war remains uncertain, but our commodity strategists expect 
Brent oil prices to remain near $100/bbl over the next few months before easing 
gradually through 2027 to $80/bbl.  In that scenario, we estimate that two-thirds of the 
cumulative passthrough from higher commodity prices to core PCE prices is behind us, 
and that the impact on year-over-year core PCE inflation will rise from 0.5pp now to a 
peak of 0.6pp in coming months before declining to 0.1pp by the end of 2027.

The near-term outlook for AI price pressures is also quite uncertain, but our equity 
analysts expect memory price growth to slow sharply in 2027 as new production 
capacity boosts supply, and this should also restrain inflation for other consumer

electronics.  We estimate that AI price pressures are currently boosting year-over-year

and shelter, which is still running above the alternative data leading indicators of rent

The Fed Outlook: Two More Hikes in December and March, Followed by 
Cuts Starting in November 
Under our economic forecast, the FOMC would remain firmly focused on the inflation 
side of its mandate in 2027.  We expect two more 25bp rate hikes in December 2026 
and March 2027 for a total of three to a peak of 4.25-4.5%, though a third hike could 
come earlier in January or the FOMC could deliver more than three hikes if the next two 
inflation reports do not look better, as we expect.  We expect the FOMC to begin a series 
of gradual cuts back to 3.25-3.5% starting at the final meeting of 2027 in November (the 
meeting was previously scheduled for December) once inflation has declined.  Both our 
baseline and probability-weighted Fed forecasts are less hawkish than market pricing.

### Page 14

The FOMC will also consider the conclusions of the task forces next year.  We expect any 
changes to be limited.  The balance sheet task force is unlikely to lead to a large 
reduction in the size of the Fed’s balance sheet, though a shift in the composition of its 
assets toward bills is possible.  The communications task force could lead to changes to 
the Summary of Economic Projections, but we would be surprised if it were eliminated 
entirely because this would seem like a large step away from transparency.  The inflation 
frameworks task force could engage with a range of questions, from how to respond to 
inflation caused by supply shocks to what the best measure of inflation is for the Fed.  
We do not expect major policy changes to come out of the alternative data or 
productivity and jobs task forces. 
Risks to Our Forecast of Sturdy Growth and Lower Inflation in 2027 
The most obvious risk to our forecast of sturdy growth and a long-awaited decline in 
inflation is that the inflation shocks of the last two years, which we and most forecasters 
did not anticipate, could last longer, or new shocks could arise.  But our best guess is that 
there will be fewer big policy-driven macroeconomic shocks in 2027, and it would be a 
fairly high bar for, say, new tariffs or a reescalation of the war with Iran to have as large 
of an impact in rate of change terms as they did over the last two years. 
We also see several downside risks to growth.  Further, increases in oil prices would 
weigh on real income growth and consumption.  A longer period of higher interest rates 
could also weigh on growth and reignite fiscal concerns, though our strategists’ forecasts 
imply a partial reversal of the recent tightening in financial conditions in 2027.  Job 
displacement from AI could come earlier and faster than we expect.  And AI could also 
introduce new risks, such as a proliferation of cyberattacks enabled by advanced AI 
tools.  But most of these risks seem more likely to have moderate rather than 
recessionary effects.

產業聚焦降息前的美國汽車產業投資布局
2026/10/30
• 美國傳統車商迎來皮卡改款
週期
• 高油價使油電、電動車需求
提升
• 自駕車、軟體營收增加帶動
車商獲利
美國傳統車商迎來皮卡改款週期，獲利表現有望提升
美國傳統車廠近年轉向生產更多高價卡車和SUV，因部分SUV和皮卡的調整後息稅前利潤率高達20%、超
過其他車型，也使美國新車平均交易價格在9月超過5萬美元。福特與通用汽車即將在2026年年底至2027
年迎來全尺寸皮卡改款與更新計畫，由於在推出新款或新一代卡車後，通常會有2~3年較佳的銷售期，預
估福特和通用汽車在2027年及之後的獲利有機會持續提升。
美國政策轉為支持燃油車發展，但高油價使油電、電動車需求提升

儘管美國政策轉向支持燃油車發展，但自美伊戰爭後油價居高不下，使油電混合車3Q26在美國的市佔率
暴衝至16%，包括豐田、現代等品牌在美國的市占率持續上升。美國3Q26電動車銷量約佔新車總銷量
6.5%，高於2Q26的5.8%，顯示電動車在美國市場正重新站穩腳步。由於油價上升使全球對電動車需求
增加，特斯拉2Q26、3Q26交車量也優於市場預期。
自駕車接受度提升，軟體營收增加帶動車商獲利
美國消費者對自動駕駛的認識與接受度正持續提升，而各車商正積極擴大自駕與軟體訂閱服務布局。通用
汽車預估其OnStar和Super Cruise自駕輔助營收在2026年將突破30億美元，福特汽車的軟體付費訂閱用
戶已達160萬戶。3Q26特斯拉FSD活躍用戶數已達174萬、年增67%，且北美新車FSD採用率超過75%。
相較傳統車商低於10%的硬體銷售利潤率，軟體毛利率高達50~70%，且訂閱制使車商部分營收轉為持續
性收入，將帶動其估值表現。
